W e argue that allo cator designs ha v eb e e n

more imp ortan t/;; higher/-lev el str ate gic issues

/2/./1 In ternal and External F ragmen tation
T raditionally /, fragmen tation is classed as external or

External fragmen tation arises when free blo c ks of

a p o or /t b ecause the blo c k is larger than needed/. In
some allo cators/, the remainder is simply w asted/, caus/-

cause the w asted memory is inside an allo cated blo c k/,

rather than b eing recorded as a free blo c ki ni t s o wn
righ t/./)
T o com bat in ternal fragmen tation/, most allo cators

will also c o alesc e adjacen t free blo c ks /(i/.e/./, neigh bo r /-
ing free blo c ks in address order/)/, com bining them in to
larger blo c ks that can b e used to satisfy requests for

Betteridge /[Bet/8/2/] attempted to compute frag/-
men tation probabilities for di/eren t allo cators using
/rst/-order Mark o v mo deling/. /(This b o ok is apparen tly
Betteridge/'s dissertation/, completed in /1/9/7/9/./) The ba/-

the lik eliho o d of di/eren t degrees of fragmen tation/.
Unfortunately /, the n um b er of p ossible states of
memory is exp onen tial in the size of memory /,a n d

T o allo w the use of a /rst/-order Mark o v mo del/, Bet/-

/5/./3 Mec hanisms
Man y curren t allo cator p olicies are partly artifacts

Deferred coalescing allo ws optimization of common
patterns of short/-term memory use/, so that scalable
mec hanisms don/'t incur high o v erheads in practice/.
The tec hniques for deferred coalescing m ust b e stud/-
ied carefully /,h o w ev er/, to ensure that this mec ha/-
nism do esn/'t degrade memory usage unacceptably b y
c hanging placemen t p olicies and undermining strate/-
gies/.

/5/./6 Challenges and Opp ortunit ie s

/| Donald Knuth /, quoted in /[Har/9/5 /]

harb ored b y the programmi ng language comm unit y /,
